\displaystyle F_{n,t}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\|G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}))\|_{m}^{2}-2\cdot\langle G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}))-G(\cdot|\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s_{i},a_{i}))\rangle_{m}\bigg\},

\displaystyle{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\|G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}))\|_{m}^{2}-2\cdot\langle G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}))-G(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}))\rangle_{m}\bigg\}.

Based on our derivations for
F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
, we can see
F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})=F_{n,t}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. Likewise, we have
{\hat{F}_{n,t}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})={\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. Then, we have the following based on our derivations for (NS2),

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})=F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})

\displaystyle=\bigg\{F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg\}+\bigg\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|F_{n,t}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big|+\big|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\big\{F_{n,t}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big\}-\big\{F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big\}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\cdot\bigg|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big|+2\cdot\big|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big|.

Using subgaussianity that we have shown for (NS1), since subgaussian constant does not depend on
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
, we can apply Theorem 8.1.6 of
[
55
]
(probabilistic tail for Dudley’s inequality) to obtain probabilistic convergence of the term
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|
. Regarding the second term, we have

\displaystyle|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|=

\displaystyle 2\cdot\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\big\langle G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i})),G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i}))\big\rangle_{m}-G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i})),G(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(R_{i},S_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}))\bigg\}\bigg|.

Letting
X_{i}=\langle G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i})),G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i}))\rangle_{m}
, we have
|X_{i}|\leq C_{max,m}^{2}
by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. Then, we can apply Hoeffiding’s inequality for bounded random variables (e.g., Theorem 2.2.6 of
[
55
]
) to obtain probabilistic bound that does not depend on
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
.

(NS4) is straightforward. However, the two statements of (
20
) need to be assumed.

C.8
Proof for Corollary
B.1

For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=(l_{2}^{2},\mathbb{W}_{1},\mathbb{W}_{p})
, we have
q=p
,
\delta=\frac{1}{p}
,
l=2
,
c=1
,
C_{subg}=C
,
C_{surr}=D^{1-\frac{1}{p}}
,
\epsilon_{0}=0
.

Based on the fact that
l_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{E}

[
6
]
for
d=1
, where energy distance
\mathcal{E}={\rm MMD}_{\beta}^{2}
(
18
) with
\beta=1
, it is equivalent to let
\mathfrak{d}={\rm MMD}_{\beta=1}^{2}
.

Let us first verify (NS1). We obtain the following for
F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. Temporarily ignoring
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
, since we have
\Delta_{n}({\theta_{1}})-\Delta_{n}({\theta_{2}})=\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{1}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{1}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\}-\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{2}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{2}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\}
, we can let
\Delta_{n}(\theta)={\hat{F}_{n,t}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. We will ignore the notation
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
for the sake of convenience.

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(\theta)=2\cdot\Delta_{n}^{(1)}(\theta)+\Delta_{n}^{(2)}(\theta),\quad\text{where }

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}^{(1)}(\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{f_{i}^{\theta}-\mathbb{E}(f_{1}^{\theta})\bigg\},\quad\Delta_{n}^{(2)}(\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{g_{i}^{\theta}-\mathbb{E}(g_{1}^{\theta})\bigg\}.

where
f_{i}^{\theta}=\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-r_{i}-\gamma\cdot Z_{\beta}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\prime};{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\|
and
g_{i}^{\theta}=\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-Z_{\beta}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)\|
, with
A_{i}^{\prime}\sim\pi(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime})
.
Let us only deal with the first term, since the second term can be handled via analogous approach. Letting
x_{i}^{\theta}:=f_{i}^{\theta}-\mathbb{E}(f_{i}^{\theta})
, we have
\Delta_{n}^{(1)}(\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}
.

In order to derive its probabilistic bound, we shall bound the following term,

\displaystyle\big\|\Delta_{n}^{(1)}({\theta_{1}})-\Delta_{n}^{(1)}({\theta_{2}})\big\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|x_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-x_{i}^{\theta_{2}}\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2},\quad\text{where}

(27)

\displaystyle\|X\|_{{\psi_{2}(n)}}:=\inf\bigg\{t>0\ ;\ \mathbb{E}_{n}\big\{\exp(X^{2}/t^{2})\big\}\leq 2\bigg\},\quad\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}:=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\mathbf{x}\|=1}\|\langle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{x}\rangle\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}.

where the inequality holds by Proposition 2.6.1 of
[
55
]
. In defining
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}
,
X
and
\mathbf{x}
mean random variable
(d=1)
and vector (
d\geq 1
). Further using the fact that
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}
is a valid norm, we can derive the following where
\mathbb{E}_{n}:=\mathbb{E}(\cdots|\mathcal{Z}_{t})
,

\displaystyle\|x_{i}^{{\theta_{1}}}-x_{i}^{{\theta_{2}}}\|_{{\psi_{2}(n)}}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{i}^{{\theta_{1}}}-f_{i}^{{\theta_{2}}}\|_{{\psi_{2}(n)}}+\mathbb{E}_{n}\|f_{i}^{\theta}-f_{i}^{{\theta_{2}}}\|_{{\psi_{2}(n)}},

\displaystyle\therefore\ \|\Delta_{n}^{(1)}({\theta_{1}})-\Delta_{n}^{(1)}({\theta_{2}})\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\|f_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-f_{i}^{\theta_{2}}\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big(\|f_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-f_{i}^{\theta_{2}}\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2}\big)\bigg\},

where the last inequality is based on Proposition 2.6.1 of
[
55
]
. Based on technique suggested in Appendix A.6 of
[
25
]
, we can derive
|f_{1}^{{\theta_{1}}}-f_{1}^{{\theta_{2}}}|\leq\mathbb{W}_{1}\{\Upsilon_{{\theta_{1}}}(s_{1},a_{1}),\Upsilon_{{\theta_{2}}}(s_{1},a_{1})\}
, which leads to following based on Example 2.5.8 of
[
55
]
,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{n}^{(1)}(\theta_{1})-\Delta_{n}^{(1)}({\theta_{2}})\|_{\psi_{2}(n)}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{1}}(s_{i},a_{i}),\Upsilon_{\theta_{2}}(s_{i},a_{i}))=\frac{C}{n}\cdot\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1,n}^{2}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}).

Applying the same logic to
\Delta_{n}^{(2)}(\theta)
, we obtain
C_{subg}=C
. It is trivial to show
\Delta_{n}(\theta)
is mean-zero.

(NS2) can be shown as follows

\displaystyle F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s_{i},a_{i})).

Here,
\mathcal{E}
is energy disance defined as
\mathcal{E}(P,Q):=2\mathbb{E}\|X-Y\|-\mathbb{E}\|X-X^{\prime}\|-\mathbb{E}\|Y-Y^{\prime}\|
for
X,X^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}Q
and
Y,Y^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}P
. Using the following lemma (proof in Appendix
D.3
), we obtain the desired result with
\lambda=2/(b-a)
.

Lemma C.7
.

Assume
1\leq p\leq r
, and let
P,Q
be probability measures for arbitrary random vectors of
d\geq 1
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(P,Q)\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{W}_{p}(P,Q)\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{W}_{r}(P,Q).

If
P,Q\in\mathcal{P}[a,b]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(b-a)^{2r-1}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{r}^{2r}(P,Q)\leq\frac{2}{(b-a)^{2p-1}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{p}^{2p}(P,Q)\leq\mathcal{E}(P,Q).

(NS3) can be shown by following.
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}
is equivalent to
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
where
{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
is defined as follows (instead of what we defined in Appendix
C.7.2
),

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-R_{i}-\gamma\cdot Z_{\beta}(S_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\prime};{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\|-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-Z_{\beta}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)\|,

with
R_{i},S_{i}^{\prime}\sim{\tilde{p}}(\cdots|s_{i},a_{i})
. Then we have following with the same logic shown in Appendix
C.7.2
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1,n,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})

\displaystyle\lesssim F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg|+2\cdot\bigg|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg|.

Just as Appendix
C.7.2
, we can show
|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|\rightarrow^{P}0
by applying Dudley’s inequality (Theorem 8.1.6 of
[
55
]
) and show
|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}^{\prime}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|\rightarrow^{P}0
with Hoeffding’s inequality for bounded random variables (Theorem 2.2.6 of
[
55
]
).

Now let us show (NS4). Every
\mu\in\Delta([a,b])
has a corresponding function function
F\in\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{F}
represents the cdf family. We let
\mathcal{F}_{\Theta}:=\{F_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{S\times A}}\ |\ F_{\theta}(s,a)\text{ is the cdf of }\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\}
, and we have
|F_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)|\leq 1
. Based on
\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L_{1}}
with functional norm
\|F\|_{L_{p}}:=(\int|F(z)|^{p}\mathrm{d}z)^{1/p}
, we can define extended norms (
21
) with
\|\cdot\|_{m}=\|\cdot\|_{L_{1}}
.

Lastly, by Section 2.3 of
[
42
]
, we have
\mathbb{W}_{p}\leq D^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1}^{1/p}
, where
D
indicates the diameter of the support of return distribution (
D=b-a
in 1-dimensional case). This leads to
C_{surr}=(b-a)^{1-1/p}
and
\delta=1/p
. Then, by applying Theorem
C.4
. we obtain the following bound for
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
,

\displaystyle\frac{(b-a)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot\bigg\{C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\}^{\frac{1/p}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}.

C.9
Proof of Corollary
B.2

For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{\beta}^{2},{\rm MMD}_{\beta},{\rm MMD}_{\beta})
, we have
q=1
,
\delta=1
,
l=2
,
c=\frac{\beta}{2}
,
C_{subg}=C\cdot(r_{\rm max}+z_{\rm max})^{\beta/2}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}C_{max}^{(\beta)}
with
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|\leq z_{\rm max}
and
\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|\leq r_{\rm max}
,
C_{surr}=1
,
\epsilon_{0}=0
. We need
\beta>1
for
q=1
as we mentioned in (
17
).

Any pair of
(\mathfrak{d},m)=({\rm MMD}^{2},{\rm MMD})
satisfies NS Properties ((NS1)–(NS4)) for bounded kernels (see Appendix
C.7.2
). Then, we can apply Theorem
C.4
to obtain the following bound for
\overline{{\rm MMD}}_{\beta,d_{\pi},1}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
.

\displaystyle\frac{C_{cover}^{1/2}}{1-\gamma^{\frac{\beta-1}{2}}}\cdot\bigg\{C_{max}^{(\beta)}\cdot C_{brack}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{2}{\beta-1}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}

If we want to obtain the result for
\beta=1
, we let
c=1/2
,
q=1+\epsilon_{N}
for some monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon_{N}\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and
C_{max}^{(\beta=1)}=C\cdot(r_{\rm max}+z_{\rm max})^{1/2}
. Applying this to Theorem
C.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{{\rm MMD}}_{1,d_{\pi},1}

\displaystyle(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\overline{{\rm MMD}}_{1,d_{\pi},q}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{C_{maxim}^{1-\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg\{C_{max}^{(\beta=1)}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1/(1+\epsilon_{N})}{2+\alpha}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1/(1+\epsilon_{N})}{1+\alpha/2}}}_{\text{constant part}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\underbrace{\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{N})}}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2(1+\epsilon_{N})}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1/(1+\epsilon_{N})}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}}_{\text{convergent part}}.

Letting
y_{N}:=\frac{\epsilon_{N}}{2(1+\epsilon_{N})}\rightarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1/y_{N}}{1/(1-\gamma^{y_{N}})}=\lim_{x\rightarrow 0}\frac{1/x}{1/(1-\gamma^{x})}=\lim_{x\rightarrow 0}\frac{1-\gamma^{x}}{x}=\log(1/\gamma).

(28)

Therefore, the convergent part can be bounded by constant multiplied by following for sufficiently large
N
,

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{N}}\bigg)^{1+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\cdot(\log N)^{1+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot(\log N)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}}\cdot O_{P}(N^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\epsilon_{N}}})

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\bigg)^{1+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\cdot(\log N)^{1+\frac{2}{2+\alpha}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{\log N}{N}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\bigg)^{1+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{(\log N)^{1+\frac{3}{2+\alpha}}\cdot N^{-\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\},

where the second last line holds by following.

\displaystyle N^{\frac{1}{1+1/\log N}}>\frac{N}{\log N}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if and only if}\quad\frac{1}{1+1/\log N}\cdot\log N>\log N-\log(\log N)

\displaystyle\text{if and only if}\quad\log(\log N)>\frac{1}{1+1/\log N}.

(29)

Then, we finally have

\displaystyle\overline{{\rm MMD}}_{1,d_{\pi},1}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})

\displaystyle\leq C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg\{C_{max}^{(\beta=1)}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{2+\alpha}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\bigg)^{1+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg((\log N)^{1+\frac{3}{2+\alpha}}\cdot N^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

C.10
Proof for Corollary
B.4

For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=(d_{L_{2}}^{2},d_{L_{2}},\mathbb{W}_{p})
, we have
q=p
,
\delta=\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}
,
l=2
,
c=1
,
C_{subg}=C\cdot(1+\gamma^{-d/2})\cdot C_{L_{2}}
,
C_{surr}=2\cdot(\max\{V_{d},1\})^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot M_{r}^{\frac{(d+2p)r}{(d+2r)p}}
,
\epsilon_{0}=0
. Here, we have
C_{L_{2}}:=\sup_{s,a}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\|f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{L_{2}}
,
M_{r}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\{\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|^{r}\}^{1/r}<\infty
and
V_{d}
is the volume of a unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Due to Appendix
C.7.2
, it suffices to show (IPS1) and (IPS2) of Definition
C.13
and (
11
). IPS properties are trivial to show.

(
11
) can be shown as follows.
Letting
f(\cdot|s,\pi):=\int_{a\in\mathcal{A}}f(\cdot|s,a)\mathrm{d}\pi(a|s)
,
we have
\|1/\gamma^{d}\cdot f_{{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}}((\cdot-r)/\gamma|s^{\prime},\pi)\|_{L_{2}}=1/\gamma^{d/2}\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\|f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{L_{2}}
. Thus we have
C_{max,m}=C\cdot(1+\gamma^{-d/2})\cdot C_{L_{2}}
. We can see
C_{L_{2}}<\infty
by following, by letting
L:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{z,s,a}|f_{\theta}(z|s,a)|<\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\|f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{f_{\theta}(z|s,a)}{L}\bigg)^{2}\mathrm{d}z\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{f_{\theta}(z|s,a)}{L}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{L},\quad\therefore\ C_{L_{2}}\leq L^{1/2}.

Lastly, by Proposition 13 of
[
54
]
, we have following under
1\leq p<r

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\leq 2\cdot(\max\{V_{d},1\})^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot M_{r}^{\frac{(d+2p)r}{(d+2r)p}}\cdot\big\{d_{L_{2}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\big\}^{\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}},

with
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}\sim\mu_{1}}\{\|\mathbf{X}\|^{r}\}^{1/r},\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}\sim\mu_{2}}\{\|\mathbf{X}\|^{r}\}^{1/r}\leq M
and
V_{d}
being the volume of
d
-dimensional unit ball. Thus, we have
\delta=\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}
with

\displaystyle C_{surr}=2\cdot(\max\{V_{d},1\})^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot M_{r}^{\frac{(d+2p)r}{(d+2r)p}}.

Applying Theorem
C.4
gives us the following bound for
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot\big(\max\{V_{d},1\}\big)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot M_{r}^{\frac{(d+2p)r}{(d+2r)p}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{r-p}{(d+2r)p}}\times\bigg\{C_{L_{2}}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p\cdot(2+\alpha)}\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}}\bigg\}

C.11
Proof of Corollary
B.5

NS Properties ((NS1)–(NS4) of Assumption
C.2
) are satisfied by Appendix
C.7.2
, since
k_{\nu}
is bounded. For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{\nu}^{2},{\rm MMD}_{\nu},\mathbb{W}_{p})
, we have
q=p
,
l=2
,
c=1
,
C_{subg}=C_{max}^{(k)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}C_{max}^{(\nu)}
. Here, we have
C_{max}^{(\nu)}<\infty
since
k_{\nu}
is a bounded function with a fixed value of
{\sigma_{\rm Mat}}>0
. Assuming that
\|f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{H^{\nu+d/2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<B
and
M_{r}<\infty
, Theorem 15 and Example 4 of
[
54
]
gives us
C_{surr}=C(B,r,M_{r},s,d,p,{\sigma_{\rm Mat}})
,
\epsilon_{0}=0
,
\delta=\frac{r-p}{(d+2r)p}
in Assumption
3.2
. See the detailed form of
C(B,r,M_{r},s,d,p,{\sigma_{\rm Mat}})>0
in their Theorem 15. Then, applying Theorem
C.4
gives us following bound for
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
,

\displaystyle\frac{C(B,r,M_{r},s,d,p,{\sigma_{\rm Mat}})}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{r-p}{2(d+2r)p}}\cdot\bigg\{C_{max}^{(\nu)}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{r-p}{(d+2r)p}}

\displaystyle\times O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{r-p}{(d+2r)p}}\bigg\}.

C.12
Proofs based on Gaussian regularizer

C.12.1
Bounding Wasserstein inaccuracy with Gaussian regularizer

Here, we introduce a method by which we can bound
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}
-inaccuracy based on regularizer
\alpha_{\sigma}(\cdot)
(see Definition 20 of
[
54
]
). In the following lemma, we use gaussian regularizer
\alpha_{\sigma}
due to its convenience, however it can be extended to other regularizers (see their Lemma 21). Since our surrogate metric
m
may depend on the value of
\sigma>0
, we will denote it as
m_{\sigma}
.

Lemma C.8
.

Let
\alpha_{\sigma}(\cdot)
be the density (or probability measure) of
N(\mathbf{0},\sigma^{2}I_{d})
and
*
indicate convolution. Assuming
\mathbb{W}_{p}(\mu*\alpha_{\sigma},\nu*\alpha_{\sigma})\leq C(\sigma,p)\cdot m_{\sigma}^{\delta}(\mu,\nu)
with
p\in(0,\frac{1}{p}]
and
m_{\sigma}
satisfies (NS4), letting
\mathfrak{d}=m_{\sigma}^{2}
achieves the following bound (with detailed bound in (
30
)),

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\lesssim\frac{C(\sigma,p)}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-\delta^{\prime}}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}+\underbrace{\frac{2^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma}_{\text{irreducible for fixed }\sigma>0.}.

Here is the proof. By Lemma 21 of
[
54
]
, we have the following,

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p}(\mu,\nu)\leq\mathbb{W}(\mu*\alpha_{\sigma},\nu*\alpha_{\sigma})+2\cdot\bigg(\int\|\mathbf{z}\|^{p}\alpha_{\sigma}(\mathbf{z})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\bigg)^{1/p}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p}(\mu*\alpha_{\sigma},\nu*\alpha_{\sigma})+2^{3/2}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma\leq C(\sigma,p)\cdot\bar{m}_{\sigma,d_{\pi},1}^{\delta}(\mu,\nu)+2^{3/2}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma.

Then, we can apply Assumption
3.2
with
C_{surr}=C(\sigma,p)
, and
\epsilon_{0}=2^{3/2}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma
. Further letting
m=m_{\sigma}
,
l=2
,
q=p
,
c=1
, we can apply Theorem
C.4
to obtain the following,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C(\sigma,p)\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}}\cdot\bigg(C_{subg}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{1+\alpha/2}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-\delta^{\prime}}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot 2^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma.

(30)

C.12.2
Proof for Corollary
B.6

The case
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{\sigma_{\rm RBF}}^{2},{\rm MMD}_{\sigma_{\rm RBF}},\mathbb{W}_{p})
satisfies Property (NS4), having
\delta=\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
,
C({\sigma_{\rm RBF}},p)=C(p,d,r,M_{r})
(see Theorem 24 of
[
54
]
for its detailed form). We also have
C_{subg}=C_{max}^{(k)}=\frac{C}{2^{d/2}\cdot\pi^{d/4}}\cdot{\sigma_{\rm RBF}}^{-d/2}
by (
26
), and
C_{brack}=C_{brack}({\sigma_{\rm RBF}})
whose dependence on
{\sigma_{\rm RBF}}>0
is not clear. Further letting
{\sigma_{\rm RBF}}=\sigma_{N}\rightarrow 0
to a monotonically decreasing sequence, applying Lemma
C.8
gives us

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{C(p,d,\sigma_{N},p)}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{r-p}{d+2r}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{C_{brack}(\sigma_{N})^{1/2}}{2^{d/2}\cdot\pi^{d/4}\cdot\sigma_{N}^{d/2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{2(r-p)}{(d+2r)p}}\bigg\}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot 2^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma_{N}.

C.12.3
Proof for Corollary
B.7

The case
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{(\sigma,f)}^{2},{\rm MMD}_{(\sigma,f)},\mathbb{W}_{p})
satisfies (NS4), having
\delta=\frac{1}{p}
,
C(\sigma,p)=2^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\sigma
by Theorem 2 of
[
68
]
, which can be applied since the kernel
k_{(\sigma,f)}
is bounded. We also have
C_{subg}=C_{max}^{(k)}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}C_{max}^{(\sigma,f)}
, which is bounded due to
k_{(\sigma,f)}(\cdot,\cdot)<\infty
by (
26
), along with
C_{brack}=C_{brack}(\sigma)
whose dependence on
\sigma>0
is not clear. Further letting
\sigma=\sigma_{N}\rightarrow 0
to a monotonically decreasing sequence, applying Lemma
C.8
gives us

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{\sigma_{N}}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\bigg(C_{max}^{(\sigma_{N},f)}\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}(\sigma_{N})\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2}^{-\alpha}}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{1/p}{1+\alpha/2}}

\displaystyle\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot 2^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma((p+d)/2)}{\Gamma(p/2)}\cdot\sigma_{N}.

Note that for fixed
\sigma>0
, we have
C_{max}^{(\sigma,f)}<\infty
as long as
k_{(\sigma,f)}(\cdot,\cdot)<\infty
holds.

C.13
Alternative for violation of (NS4)

C.13.1
Alternative properties and results

In Theorem
C.4
, (NS4) may not hold. There may not be a corresponding functional norm space (e.g., Wasserstein for
d\geq 2
) or the functional element may be unbounded, that is,
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{z,s,a}G_{\theta}(z|s,a)=\infty
. For such examples, we should replace (NS3) and (NS4) of Section
3.2
with the following (MS3) and (MS4). We can maintain (NS1) and (NS2) from NS properties, renaming them (MS1) and (MS2).

Assumption C.9

(Metric-space association)
.

The functional Bregman divergence
\mathfrak{d}
and probability metric
m
over
\mathcal{P}
form metric-space association (MS association, in short) if there exists an metric space
(\mathcal{G},m)
where
any element
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
can be uniquely (up to equivalence under zero metric
m
) represented by the corresponding element
G(\cdot|\mu)\in\mathcal{G}
such that

(MS1)

The
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
-conditioned modulus
\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}):=\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\}-\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\}
is a mean-zero sub-gaussian process with respect to
\bar{m}_{n,1}
. That is, for any
\mathcal{Z}_{t}\subset\mathcal{S\times A}
and
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}\in\Theta
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{n}({\theta_{1}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-\Delta_{n}({\theta_{2}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\|_{{\psi_{2}(n)}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot C_{subg}\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}).

(MS2)

We have
F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\geq\lambda\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}^{l}(\theta,{\theta_{*,t}})
for some
l\geq 2
. Here, the value of
\lambda>0
does not depend on
\mathcal{Z}_{t}
,
\theta
,
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
(or
{\theta_{*,t}}
such that
\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}=\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
).

(MS3)

\bar{m}_{\rho,1}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\rightarrow^{P}0
converges in a rate that does not depend on
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
(or
{\theta_{*,t}}
).

(MS4)

There exists a constant
C_{\rm met}\in(0,\infty)
and
\alpha\in(0,2)
such that

\displaystyle{\rm diam}(\Theta,m_{\infty})<\infty\quad\&\quad\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,m_{\infty},\epsilon)\leq C_{\rm met}\cdot\epsilon^{-\alpha}.

It is possible that there exists an element in
\mathcal{G}
that does not represent any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
.

Then we can propose the following lemma. Its proof is in Appendix
D.4
.

Lemma C.10
.

Assume Assumption
3.1
and
C_{3}>0
defined below has finite value. For any
\beta>0
, we have some sequence
\delta_{n}=\max\{\delta_{n}^{(1)},\delta_{n}^{(2)}\}
such that
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and following
\phi(\cdot)
mentioned in Theorem
D.1
. Here,
\delta_{n}^{(1)}
and
\delta_{n}^{(2)}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\delta_{n}^{(1)}:=\bigg(\frac{2C_{1}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)^{1/\beta}\quad\&\quad\delta\geq\delta_{n}^{(2)}\text{ implies }\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot C_{subg}\cdot C_{\rm ent}\cdot\delta^{\frac{{\alpha_{0}}}{2}\beta^{\prime}}\geq C_{3}\cdot\exp\bigg(\frac{-n\cdot\delta^{2\beta}}{4C_{2}^{2}}\bigg),

\displaystyle\phi(\delta):=C_{\phi}\cdot\delta^{\frac{{\alpha_{0}}}{2}\beta^{\prime}}\quad\text{with}\quad C_{\phi}:=C\cdot C_{subg}\cdot C_{\rm ent}\quad\&\quad\beta^{\prime}=\min\{\beta,2\},

Here are the constants:

\displaystyle C_{\rm ent}=C_{\rm met}^{1/2}/(1-\alpha/2)\quad\&\quad{\alpha_{0}}=1-\alpha/2\in(0,1),

\displaystyle C_{1}:=\frac{C}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot C_{\rm met}^{1/2}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;m_{\infty})^{2-\alpha/2}\quad\&\quad C_{2}:=C\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;m_{\infty})^{2}.

C_{3}>0
is the finite constant that uniformly bounds the modulus
{\tilde{\Delta}_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})}
with
F(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}):=\mathbb{E}_{S,A\sim\rho,R,S^{\prime}\sim{\tilde{p}}(\cdots|S,A)}(\mathfrak{d}\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(S,A),\Psi_{\pi}(R,S^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\})

\displaystyle{\tilde{\Delta}_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})}:=\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\}-\{{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\},

\displaystyle C_{3}:=\sup_{\theta,{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a,r,s^{\prime}}|{\tilde{\Delta}_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})}|<\infty.

Based on Lemma
C.10
, we obtain the following theorem, which is proved in Appendix
D.5
.

Theorem C.11
.

Suppose Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
,
C.3
, and
C_{3}<\infty
for Lemma
C.10
. Moreover, given a probability metric
\eta
that satisfies (S-L-C) with convexity parameter
q\geq 1
and scale-sensitivity parameter
c>1/(2q)
(i.e., (
16
) of Appendix
C.4
), consider the FDE with a functional Bregman divergence
\mathfrak{d}
satisfying Assumption
C.2
of Appendix
C.6
. Then, by letting
T=\lfloor\frac{1}{c-\frac{1}{2q}}\cdot\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\beta_{1}}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\rfloor
with
\delta^{\prime}=\min\{\delta,1/q\}
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{d_{\pi},q}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{c-\frac{1}{2q}}}\cdot C_{0}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\cdot\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{N}\bigg)^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2\beta_{1}}}\bigg\}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{c-\frac{1}{2q}}}\cdot 2^{1-\frac{1}{2q}}\cdot\epsilon_{0},

\displaystyle C_{0}:=C_{surr}\cdot C_{maxim}^{\max\{(1-\frac{1}{q\delta})\delta,0\}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}}\cdot C_{B}^{\prime}\quad\&\quad\beta_{1}=\max\bigg\{\frac{l}{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{4}\alpha},l-1+\frac{\alpha}{2}\bigg\}

where
C_{B}^{\prime}
is specified in (
46
). Note that we need
q>1/(2c)
to ensure
\gamma^{c-\frac{1}{2q}}\in(0,1)
.

C.13.2
Proof for Corollary
B.8

For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{cou}^{2},{\rm MMD}_{cou},\mathbb{W}_{p})
, we have
q=p
,
\delta=1/p
,
l=2
,
c=1
,
C_{subg}=C_{max}^{(k)}=C(1+\gamma^{1-d/2})\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a))\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}C_{max}^{(cou)}
,
C_{surr}=C\cdot D^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot V_{d}^{\frac{1}{2p}}
.

(MS1) and (MS2) are straightforward from Appendix
C.7.2
. Note that
C_{max}^{(k)}=C_{max}^{(cou)}
(
26
) can be derived as follows (for unbounded kernel
k_{cou}
). Based on
\|\kappa_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\langle\kappa_{\mu},\kappa_{\mu}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathbb{E}\{k_{cou}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime})\}
with
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}\mu
, we can use
\|\kappa_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}(\mu)
to obtain the following,

\displaystyle{\rm MMD}_{cou}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\Psi_{\pi}(r,s^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg\}\leq C\cdot\bigg\{\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a))+\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}\big\{\Psi_{\pi}(r,s^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big\}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}(\Psi_{\pi}(r,s^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}))\leq\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}\pi(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})\cdot\|\kappa_{(g_{r,\gamma})_{\#}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}\pi(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{E}_{cou}^{1/2}((g_{r,\gamma})_{\#}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})).

Since we have the following based on definition of
\kappa_{0}^{(cou)}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{cou}\bigg\{(g_{r,\gamma})_{\#}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}))\bigg\}=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\kappa_{0}^{(cou)}(\gamma Z_{\alpha}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime};{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-\gamma Z_{\beta}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime};{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}))\bigg\}=\gamma^{2-d}\mathcal{E}_{cou}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})).

This gives us
C_{max}^{(k)}=C_{max}^{(cou)}
.

(MS3) can be shown analogously with Section
C.8
. (MS4) regards the model complexity that we assume.
C_{3}>0
can be shown to be bounded by using
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}_{cou}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a))<\infty
, which is assumed in Corollary
B.8
.

We also obtain
C_{surr}=C\cdot D^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot V_{d}^{\frac{1}{2p}}
where
V_{d}
is the volume of a unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, by Theorem 1.1 of
[
10
]
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu,\nu)\leq C\cdot D^{1/2}\cdot V_{d}^{1/2}\cdot{\rm MMD}_{cou}(\mu,\nu),

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p}(\mu,\nu)\leq D^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1}^{1/p}(\mu,\nu)\leq C\cdot D^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot V_{d}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot{\rm MMD}_{cou}^{1/p}(\mu,\nu).

Then applying this into Theorem
C.11
gives us following bound, since we have
\beta_{1}=2/(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{4}\alpha)
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{D^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot V_{d}^{\frac{1}{2p}}}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot C_{B}^{\prime}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\cdot\bigg(\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{N}\bigg)^{\frac{6-\alpha}{16p}}\bigg\},

\displaystyle C_{B}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\bigg(\max\bigg\{C_{max}^{(cou)}\cdot C_{\rm met}^{1/2},C_{\rm met}^{1/2}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{p,\infty})^{2-\alpha/2},{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{p,\infty})^{2}\bigg\}\bigg)^{\frac{6-\alpha}{8p}},

where
C_{B}^{\prime}
is defined accordingly to (
46
).

C.13.3
Proof for Corollary
B.3

For the case of
(\mathfrak{d},m,\eta)=({\rm MMD}_{\beta=1}^{2},\mathbb{W}_{1},\mathbb{W}_{1})
, we have
q=1
,
\delta=1
,
l=\frac{r(2d+3)-1}{r-1}
,
c=1
,
C_{subg}=C
,
C_{surr}=1
,
\epsilon_{0}=0
. We need
\beta>1
for
q=1
as we mentioned in (
17
).

(MS1) holds, as we have already shown in Appendix
C.8
. This can be copied since
l_{2}^{2}
is a special case of
{\rm MMD}_{\beta=1}^{2}
for
d=1
. However, (MS2) should be changed as follows, based on Proposition 17 of
[
34
]
: when
M_{r}<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})\cdot{\rm MMD}_{\beta}^{\rho^{\prime}}(\mu,\nu)\geq\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu,\nu)\quad\text{with }\rho^{\prime}=\frac{r-1}{r(d+\beta/2+1)-\beta/2}.

(31)

Refer to their Proposition 17 for the definition of constant
C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})>0
. This can be developed into

\displaystyle F_{n,t}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F_{n,t}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})=\overline{{\rm MMD}}_{\beta,n,1}^{2}(\theta,{\theta_{*,t}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\rm MMD}_{\beta}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i}))

\displaystyle\geq C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})^{-2/\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i}))\bigg\}^{1/\rho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})^{-2/\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i}),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i}))\bigg\}^{1/\rho^{\prime}}\quad\because\ \frac{1}{\rho^{\prime}}>2

\displaystyle=C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})^{-2/\rho^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1,n,1}^{2/\rho^{\prime}}(\theta,{\theta_{*,t}}).

Since we are limiting to
\beta=1
, (MS2) is satisfied with
\lambda=C(d,r,\beta,M_{r})^{-2/\rho^{\prime}}
and
l=\frac{2}{\rho^{\prime}}=\frac{r(2d+3)-1}{r-1}\geq 2d+3
.

(MS3) can be shown analogously to
C.8
. In the part of applying Hoeffding’s inequality (generalized version for unbounded distributions, e.g., Theorem 2.6.2), we should assume
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|<\infty
and
\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
.

(MS4) is what we assume. Finiteness of
C_{3}>0
can be shown by using the same trick that holds between energy distance and Wassertein-1 metric (as we mentioned in Appendix
C.8
),

\displaystyle|{\tilde{\Delta}_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})}|

\displaystyle\leq|{\hat{F}_{n,t}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-{\hat{F}_{n,t}}({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|+|F(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})-F({\theta_{*,t}}|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f_{i}^{\theta}-f_{i}^{\theta_{*,t}})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(g_{i}^{\theta}-g_{i}^{\theta_{*,t}})\bigg|+\bigg|2\cdot\mathbb{E}(f_{1}^{\theta}-f_{1}^{\theta_{*,t}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{1}^{\theta}-g_{1}^{\theta_{*,t}})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 8\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{W}(\theta;{\theta_{*,t}})\leq 8\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})<\infty\quad\text{by (MS4).}

Then, we can apply Theorem
C.11
to obtain the following bound, since
\beta_{1}=l-1+\alpha/2
.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1,d_{\pi},1}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{1/2}}\cdot C_{cover}^{1/2}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{1}{\log(1/\gamma)}\cdot\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{N}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2(l-1)+\alpha}}\bigg\},

\displaystyle C_{B}^{\prime}:=\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\max\bigg\{1,C_{\rm met}^{1/2}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})^{2-\alpha/2},{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})^{\frac{l-1+\alpha/2}{2}}\bigg\}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{l-1+\alpha/2}}.

C.14
Proof for Corollary
B.9

We acknowledge that the following proof is based upon Chapter 7 of
[
19
]
and Section 6.4 of
[
48
]
. Throughout this proof, we will resort to the following trick shown by
[
48
]
,

\displaystyle|\sqrt{2s}-\sqrt{s+t}|\leq|\sqrt{s}-\sqrt{t}|\leq(1+\sqrt{2})\cdot|\sqrt{2s}-\sqrt{s+t}|\quad\text{for }\forall s,t\geq 0.

Now, let us define the following functions. Define
\mathbb{E}_{s,a}
to be expectation taken with respect to
s,a\sim\rho
,
\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}}
to be expectation taken with respect to
r,s^{\prime}\sim{\tilde{p}}(\cdots|s,a)
,
\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdots|s^{\prime},\pi)}
to be expectation taken with respect to
z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s^{\prime},\pi)
, where
f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s^{\prime},\pi):=\int f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\pi(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle M(\theta):=\mathbb{E}_{s,a}\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdots|s^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{f_{\theta}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\},

\displaystyle{\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{z_{i}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{f_{\theta}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})}\bigg\}.

Note that it is more appropriate to write it as
M(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
and
{\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. However, we will take
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}\in\Theta
(along with the corresponding
{\theta_{*,t}}
) as given, and simplify it as
M(\theta)=M(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
and
{\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta)={\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
. Since the inner part of
\mathbb{E}_{s,a}
of
M(\theta)
is equivalent to
\text{KLD}(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)\|\frac{f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)}{2})\geq 0
where equality holds under
\theta={\theta_{*,t}}
,
{\theta_{*,t}}
becomes the minimizer of
M(\theta)
with
M({\theta_{*,t}})=0
.

C.14.1
Quadratic lower bound

For fixed
s,a
, denote
p_{0}(\cdot)=f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)
and
p(\cdot)=f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)
. Then, the terms inside
\mathbb{E}_{s,a}
is simplified as follows with
q=\frac{1}{2}(p+p_{0})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{z\sim p_{0}}\bigg\{-\log\frac{p(z)+p_{0}(z)}{2p_{0}(z)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\bigg\}=\mathbb{E}_{z\sim p_{0}}\bigg\{-\log\frac{q(z)}{p_{0}(z)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq 2\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{q(z)}{p_{0}(z)}}\bigg)p_{0}(z)\mathrm{d}z\qquad\because\ \frac{1}{2}\log x<\sqrt{x}-1

\displaystyle=2\cdot\bigg(1-\int_{p_{0}>0}\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\cdot\sqrt{q(z)}\mathrm{d}z\bigg)\geq\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(p_{0}(z)+q(z)-2\cdot\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\cdot\sqrt{q(z)}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{q(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{2p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{(p_{0}+p)(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{p(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot h_{0}^{2}(p_{0},p),

where
h_{0}^{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{p_{0}>0}(\sqrt{f_{1}(z)}-\sqrt{f_{2}(z)})^{2}\mathrm{d}z
where
f_{1},f_{2}
are corresponding pdf’s of
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}
. Allowing abuse of notation, we will allow putting densities instead of probabilty measures for the inputs.

Then, since
M({\theta_{*,t}})=0
, we have the following where
\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}
is defined accordingly to (
8
),

\displaystyle M(\theta)-M({\theta_{*,t}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s,a}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{f_{\theta}(z|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\bigg\{\frac{1}{(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot h_{0}^{2}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a),f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)\big)\bigg\}=\frac{1}{(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}^{2}({\theta_{*,t}},\theta).

Here we have
\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}^{2}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}):=\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\{h_{0}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{1}}(s,a),\Upsilon_{\theta_{2}}(s,a))\}
with
h_{0}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{1}}(s,a),\Upsilon_{\theta_{2}}(s,a))
takes the integral over the support of
\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a)
, or
f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)>0
.

C.14.2
Convergence of modulus

We recall the following Theorem 6.13 of
[
48
]
.

Theorem C.12
.

For any class
\mathcal{F}
of measurable functions
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
\|f\|_{P_{0},B}\leq\delta
, with
X_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}P_{0}
, we have following with
\lesssim
indicating “bounded by some multiplicative constant,”

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}f(X_{i})\bigg|\bigg\}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})\cdot\bigg(1+\frac{{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{n}}\bigg),

\displaystyle{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|):=\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}\cup\{0\},\|\cdot\|)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{1+\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{P_{0},B}^{2}:=2\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{0}}\big\{\exp(|f(X)|)-1-|f(X)|\big\}.

Let us define the following.

\displaystyle m_{\theta}(s,a,r,s^{\prime}):=\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{f_{\theta}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\},

\displaystyle\therefore\ {\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{m_{\theta}(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})-m_{\theta_{*,t}}(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\big\},

\displaystyle M(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{s,a,r,s^{\prime}}\big\{m_{\theta}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})\big\}=\mathbb{E}\big\{m_{\theta}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})-m_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})\big\}.

We also define
{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}}:=\{m_{\theta}(\cdots)\ :\ \theta\in\Theta\}=\{m_{\theta}(\cdots)-m_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdots)\ :\ \theta\in\Theta\}
, since we have
m_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdots)=0
. Since
m_{\theta}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})\leq\log 2
and
x<\log 2
implies
\exp(|x|)-1-|x|\leq 4\cdot(\exp(-x/2)-1)^{2}
, we have the following,

\displaystyle\|m_{\theta}\|_{P_{0},B}^{2}\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s,a}\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}}\bigg\{4\cdot\bigg(\exp\big(-m_{\theta}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})/2\big)-1\bigg)^{2}\bigg\}=

\displaystyle 8\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s,a)}\bigg\{\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{f_{\theta}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)}{f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r+\gamma z^{\prime}|s,a)}}\bigg)^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\}.

Note that
\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}\sim{\tilde{p}}(\cdots|s,a)}\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s,a)}
can be reduced into
\mathbb{E}_{z\sim f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)}
. Again, letting
p_{0}(\cdot)=f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)
and
p(\cdot)=f_{\theta}(\cdot|s,a)
for fixed
s,a
, along with
q=(p+p_{0})/2
, the term within
\mathbb{E}_{s,a}
can be rewritten as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{z\sim p_{0}}\bigg\{\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{q(z)}{p_{0}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\bigg\}=\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{q(z)}{p_{0}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}p_{0}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{q(z)}-\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{0}+p}{2}(z)}-\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\bigg)^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p_{0}+p}(z)-\sqrt{2p_{0}(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p(z)}-\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=h_{0}^{2}(p_{0},p).

Then, this leads to
\|m_{\theta}\|_{P_{0},B}\leq 2\sqrt{2}\cdot\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}({\theta_{*,t}},\theta)
, meaning that
\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}({\theta_{*,t}},\theta)\leq\frac{\delta}{2\sqrt{2}}
implies
\|m_{\theta}\|_{P_{0},B}\leq\delta
. Then, applying Theorem
C.12
gives us the following with modulus constructed as
\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})=({\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta)-M(\theta))-({\hat{M}_{n}}({\theta_{*,t}})-M({\theta_{*,t}}))
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta:\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}({\theta_{*,t}},\theta)<\frac{\delta}{2\sqrt{2}}}\big|({\hat{M}_{n}}(\theta)-M(\theta))-({\hat{M}_{n}}({\theta_{*,t}})-M({\theta_{*,t}}))\big|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})\cdot\bigg(1+\frac{{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

(32)

Now let us simplify the term
{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})
. Assume a bracketing pair
m_{p}(\cdots)\leq m_{q}(\cdots)
, which may not be elements of
{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}}
. That is, we have
p(\cdot|\cdots)\geq q(\cdot|\cdots)
(where we can assume
q(\cdot|\cdots)\geq 0
but not necessarily conditional probability densities), and

\displaystyle m_{p}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s,a)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{p(z|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\},

\displaystyle m_{q}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}_{z^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s,a)}\bigg\{-\log\frac{q(z|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big)\bigg\}.

The term
\|m_{p}(\cdots)-m_{q}(\cdots)\|_{P_{0},B}
is defined as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s,a}\mathbb{E}_{r,s^{\prime}}\bigg[\bigg\{\exp\big(|m_{p}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})-m_{q}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})|\big)-1-|m_{p}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})-m_{q}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})|\bigg\}^{2}\bigg].

Employing the same trick that we have used before, the term inside
\mathbb{E}_{s,a}
can be bounded as follows with
p_{0}(z)=f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)
for fixed
s,a
,

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot\mathbb{E}_{z\sim p_{0}}\bigg\{\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p(z)+p_{0}(z)}{q(z)+p_{0}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=4\cdot\mathbb{E}_{z\sim p_{0}}\bigg\{\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}(z)}{p_{2}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\bigg\}\quad\text{with }p_{1}=p+p_{0},\ p_{2}=q+p_{0}

\displaystyle=4\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}(z)}{p_{2}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}p_{0}(z)\mathrm{d}z\leq 4\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}(z)}{p_{2}(z)}}-1\bigg)^{2}\cdot p_{2}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=8\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p+p_{0}}{2}(z)}-\sqrt{\frac{q+p_{0}}{2}(z)}\bigg)^{2}\mathrm{d}z\leq 8\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p(z)}-\sqrt{q(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=16\cdot h_{0}^{2}(p,q)

Then, we have
\|m_{p}(\cdots)-m_{q}(\cdots)\|_{P_{0},B}\leq 4\cdot\|\sqrt{p(\cdot|\cdots)}-\sqrt{q(\cdot|\cdots)}\|_{L_{2},\rho}
.
\|\cdot\|_{L_{2},\rho}
is defined accordingly to (
21
). Thus we have the following,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}({\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B},\epsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}^{1/2},\|\cdot\|_{L_{2},\rho},4/\epsilon).

Then, this leads to following by assumption,

\displaystyle{J_{[\ ]}}(\delta,{\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}},\|\cdot\|_{P_{0},B})\leq\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}^{1/2},\|\cdot\|_{L_{2},\rho},\epsilon/2)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq C\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\delta^{1-\alpha/2}.

We will later select
\delta_{n}
such that
C\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\delta_{n}^{1-\alpha/2}=\sqrt{n}\cdot\delta_{n}^{2}
and apply Theorem
D.1
.

C.14.3
Consistence

Before we apply Theorem
D.1
, let us show
\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\rightarrow^{P}0
. By Lemma 4.2 of
[
19
]
, we have following,

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})=\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\big\{h_{0}^{2}\big(f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(\cdot|s,a),f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)\big)\big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\bigg\{h_{0}^{2}\bigg(\frac{f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}+f_{\theta_{*,t}}}{2}(\cdot|s,a),f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)\bigg)\bigg\}\leq(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}\cdot\big|M({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})-{\hat{M}_{n}}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})\big|.

The first inequality holds by following. Letting
p
be an arbitrary density and
\bar{p}=\frac{1}{2}(p+p_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle h_{0}^{2}(\bar{p},p_{0})=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p+p_{0}}{2}(z)}-\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}\bigg)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{4}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}(\sqrt{(p+p_{0})(z)}-\sqrt{2p_{0}(z)})^{2}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}(\sqrt{p(z)}-\sqrt{p_{0}(z)})^{2}\mathrm{d}z=\frac{1}{2(1+\sqrt{2})^{2}}\cdot h_{0}^{2}(p,p_{0}).

The second inequality above holds due to following. Note that
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}
is defined as follows by (
10
) with
\mathfrak{d}(P,Q)=\text{KLD}(Q\|P)
and we have following based on concavity of logarithm function, i.e.,
\log\frac{x+y}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\log x+\frac{1}{2}\log y
for
x,y>0
.

\displaystyle{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}:=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{z_{i}^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{-\log f_{\theta}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})\bigg\},

\displaystyle\log\frac{f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(z|s,a)+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(z|s,a)}{f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(z|s,a)>0\big).

Then this leads to following which justifies the inequality,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{z_{i}^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{\log f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})-\log f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{z_{i}^{\prime}\sim f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime},\pi)}\bigg\{\log\frac{f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})+f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})}{2\cdot f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(f_{\theta_{*,t}}(r_{i}+\gamma z_{i}^{\prime}|s_{i},a_{i})\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot(-{\hat{M}_{n}}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})+M({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}))+2\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s,a,r,s^{\prime}}\big\{-m_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot(-{\hat{M}_{n}}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})+M({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}))-4\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s,a}\bigg\{h_{0}^{2}\bigg(\frac{f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)+f_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(\cdot|s,a)}{2},f_{\theta_{*,t}}(\cdot|s,a)\bigg)\bigg\}.

The last line above holds, since the term
\mathbb{E}_{s,a,r,s^{\prime}}\{-m_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime})\}
can be bounded using the following fact,

\displaystyle\int\log\frac{\bar{p}(z)}{p_{0}(z)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(p_{0}(z)>0\big)\cdot p_{0}(z)\mathrm{d}z\leq 2\cdot\int\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\bar{p}(z)}{p_{0}(z)}}-1\bigg)p_{0}(z)\mathrm{d}z\quad\because\ \frac{1}{2}\log x\leq\sqrt{x}-1

\displaystyle=2\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}(\sqrt{\bar{p}(z)}\cdot\sqrt{p_{0}(z)})\mathrm{d}z-2\leq-\int_{p_{0}>0}\big(\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{\bar{p}(z)}\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}z=-2\cdot h_{0}^{2}(\bar{p},p_{0}).

Using
|M({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})-{\hat{M}_{n}}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}})|=\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})
since
{\hat{M}_{n}}({\theta_{*,t}})=M({\theta_{*,t}})=0
, we can show the following by Markov’s inequality and (
32
), with probability bound not depending on
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})|\geq\epsilon\big)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\Delta_{n}(\theta|{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big|\bigg\}\rightarrow^{P}0,

as long as
{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}^{1/2},\|\cdot\|_{L_{2},\rho})<\infty
.

C.14.4
Finalization

As we mentioned at the end of Appendix
C.14.2
, we let

\displaystyle\delta_{n}=\bigg(\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{n}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\quad\text{where }C_{0}=C\cdot C_{brack}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2},

\displaystyle\therefore\ \bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})=C_{brack}^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot O_{P}\big(n^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}}\big).

Now let us bound further bound Wasserstein metric by
h_{0}
. Considering two densities
p
and
p_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{p_{0}>0}|p_{0}(z)-p(z)|\mathrm{d}z=\int_{p_{0}>0}|\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{p(z)}|\cdot|\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}+\sqrt{p(z)}|\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\int_{p_{0}>0}|\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}-\sqrt{p(z)}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\cdot\bigg(\int_{p_{0}>0}|\sqrt{p_{0}(z)}+\sqrt{p(z)}|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}\leq 4\cdot h_{0}(p_{0},p).

This allows us to bound TVD metric as follows,

\displaystyle\int|p_{0}(z)-p(z)|\mathrm{d}z\leq\int_{p_{0}>0}|p_{0}(z)-p(z)|\mathrm{d}z+\int_{p>0}|p_{0}(z)-p(z)|\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot\int_{p_{0}>0}|p_{0}(z)-p(z)|\mathrm{d}z\leq 12\cdot h_{0}(p_{0},p).

Based on
\mathbb{W}_{p}(P,Q)\leq D\cdot\text{TVD}^{1/p}(P,Q)
shown in Lemma C.6 of
[
61
]
, this leads to
\mathbb{W}_{p}(p_{0},p)\leq C\cdot D\cdot h_{0}^{1/p}(p_{0},p)
. This leads to following,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot D\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\bar{h}_{0,\rho,1}^{1/p}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot D\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot C_{brack}^{\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{1/p}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot O_{P}\big(n^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}}\big).

Letting
T=\lfloor\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\rfloor
, we apply
n=N/T
based on (
6
), and we obtain the following bound for
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
,

\displaystyle\frac{D}{1-\gamma^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\cdot C_{cover}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot C_{brack}^{\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\alpha/2}\bigg)^{\frac{1/p}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\bigg)^{\frac{1/p}{2+\alpha}}\cdot O_{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{N}{\log_{1/\gamma}N}\bigg)^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}}\bigg\}.

Of course, we should show that condition of Lemma
C.6
is satisfied before we apply (
6
). Its proof is analogous to Appendix
D.5.2
that we used in proving Theorem
C.11
.

C.15
Proof for Theorem
3.3

Definition C.13

(Inner-product-space metric)
.

The probability metric
m
over
\mathcal{P}
is called
an inner-product-space metric (IPS metric, in short) if there exists an inner product space
(\mathcal{G},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{m})
where
any element
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
can be uniquely (up to equivalence under zero induced norm
\|\cdot\|_{m}
) represented by the corresponding element
G(\cdot|\mu)\in\mathcal{G}
such that

(IPS1)

The probability
m
is expressed as the norm of difference between functional representers, i.e.,
m(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}):=\|G(\cdot|\mu_{1})-G(\cdot|\mu_{2})\|_{m}
,
where
\|\cdot\|_{m}
is the induced norm by the inner product;

(IPS2)

Functional representers of probability measures are unbiased with respect to probability mixture. That is, for
\mu(\cdot):\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}
and arbitrary probability
P
over
\mathcal{Z}
, we have
G(\cdot|\int\mu(z)\mathrm{d}P(z))=\int G(\cdot|\mu(z))\mathrm{d}P(z)
.

It is possible that there exists an element in
\mathcal{G}
that does not represent any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
.

Assuming
|\mathcal{S\times A}|<\infty
, each state-action pair can be observed with probability at least
{p_{\rm min}}:=\inf_{s,a}\rho(s,a)
where
\rho(s,a)=\mathbb{P}\{S,A=s,a\}
. We can always assume
{p_{\rm min}}>0
without loss of generality, since we can exclude
s,a
with
\rho(s,a)>0
from
\mathcal{S\times A}
. Denoting the empirical estimate of
\rho(s,a)
and
{\tilde{p}}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
as
{\hat{\rho}}(s,a)
and
\hat{p}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
, we can copy the logic of (
14
) to obtain the following equivalence for
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}
defined in (
10
),

\displaystyle{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}{\sum_{s,a}}{\hat{\rho}}(s,a)\cdot\mathfrak{d}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}}(s,a)\bigg\}.

Here,
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
is defined accordingly to (
3
), only replacing
{\tilde{p}}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
with
\hat{p}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
.

Now, we restrict
\mathfrak{d}=m^{2}
into squared form of probability metric so that it satisfies relaxed triangular inequality (i.e.,
\mathfrak{d}(P,Q)\leq C\cdot(\mathfrak{d}(P,R)+\mathfrak{d}(R,Q))
for some
C\geq 1
. With fixed
{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
and its corresponding value fo
{\theta_{*,t}}
such that
\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}=\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
, we have following,

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}.{\theta_{*,t}})\leq 2\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})+2\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}},\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}})

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}},\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\leq 4\cdot\sup_{s,a}m^{2}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\bigg\},

(33)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m^{2}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{1}}(s_{i},a_{i}),\Upsilon_{\theta_{2}}(s_{i},a_{i})\bigg\}.

(34)

Given that
\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}=\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}
, this comes down to showing
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)
converges in
m
towards
\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)
for each
s,a
. Towards that end, we will assume that each
s,a
is observed sufficiently many times. We define the following event along with two probability vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{S\times A}}
,

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}:=\bigg\{\omega\in\Omega\ \bigg|\ \|{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}-{\mathbf{p}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}{p_{\rm min}}\bigg\}\quad\text{with}\quad{\mathbf{p}}:=\big(\rho(s,a)\big)\quad\&\quad{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}:=\big({\hat{\rho}}(s,a)\big).

By Lemma A.3 of
[
25
]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0}^{c})=\mathbb{P}\big(\|{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}-{\mathbf{p}}\|_{\infty}>\frac{1}{2}{p_{\rm min}}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\|{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}-{\mathbf{p}}\|>\frac{1}{2}{p_{\rm min}}\big)\leq\exp(\frac{1}{4})\cdot\exp\big(\frac{-n}{128}\cdot{p_{\rm min}}^{2}\big).

Note that conditioning on
\Omega_{0}
has no effect on the randomness of
r,s^{\prime}\sim{\tilde{p}}(\cdots|s,a)
for each
s,a
. Denote its conditional expectation and probability as
{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}(\cdots):=\mathbb{E}(\cdots|\Omega_{0})
and
{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}(\cdots):=\mathbb{P}(\cdots|\Omega_{0})
. Based on the IPS Properties of Definition
C.13
and (
11
), we obtain the following for a fixed
s,a
, assuming that we have collected
\{(s,a,r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}
for the
s,a
. We acknowledge that this proof is highly based on Lemma 10 of
[
1
]
.

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[m^{2}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg]={\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[m^{2}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[\bigg\|G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))-G\bigg(\cdot\bigg|\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}\bigg)\bigg\|_{m}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[\bigg\|\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\bigg\{G\big(\cdot\big|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)-G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\bigg\}\bigg\|_{m}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[\big\|G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)-G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\big\|_{m}^{2}\bigg]+\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}^{2}}\times

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{i\neq j}{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[\bigg\langle G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)-G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a)),G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{j},s_{j}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)-G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\bigg\rangle_{m}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\bigg\{{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\|G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)\|_{m}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\|_{m}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-2\cdot{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\big\{\big\langle G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}),G(\cdot|\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\big)\big\rangle_{m}\big\}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\bigg\{{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\big\|G\big(\cdot|\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}})\big)\big\|_{m}^{2}\bigg\}\quad\because\ \Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a)=\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{max,m}}{{n(s,a)}}\leq\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{2}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot C_{max,m}\quad\because\ {n(s,a)}=n\cdot{\hat{\rho}}(s,a)>n\cdot\frac{{p_{\rm min}}}{2}\ \ \text{under }\Omega_{0}.

Then, using
\mathbb{E}(X)\leq\{\mathbb{E}(X^{2})\}^{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}\bigg[m\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\big\}\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2}{{p_{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{max,m}^{1/2}.

Temporarily making notation simplification
z_{i}=(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})
, we define

\displaystyle\phi(z_{1},\cdots,z_{n(s,a)}):=m\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\Psi_{i}\bigg\}\quad\text{where }\Psi_{i}:=\Psi_{\pi}(r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime},\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}).

Since we have following where
\Psi_{j}^{\prime}=\Psi_{j}
for
j\neq i
and
\Psi_{i}^{\prime}=\Psi(z_{i}^{\prime};\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}},\pi)
,

\displaystyle\big|\phi(z_{1},\cdots,z_{i},\cdots,z_{{n(s,a)}})-\phi(z_{1}.\cdots,z_{i}^{\prime},\cdots,z_{{n(s,a)}})\big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|m\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\Psi_{i}\bigg\}-m\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\Psi_{i}^{\prime}\bigg\}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}G(\cdot|\Psi_{i})-\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}G(\cdot|\Psi_{i}^{\prime})\bigg\|_{m}\leq\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n(s,a)}}\bigg\|G(\cdot|\Psi_{i})-G(\cdot|\Psi_{i}^{\prime})\bigg\|_{m}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{{n(s,a)}}\cdot C_{max,m}.

Then, by McDiarmid’s inequality (e.g., Theorem 2.9.1 of
[
55
]
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\bigg(\bigg|m\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s.a)\big\}\bigg|\geq t\bigg)\leq 2\cdot\exp\bigg(\frac{-2t^{2}}{\frac{1}{{n(s,a)}}\cdot 4C_{max,m}^{2}}\bigg)\leq 2\cdot\exp\bigg(\frac{-n\cdot{p_{\rm min}}\cdot t^{2}}{4C_{max,m}^{2}}\bigg).

Now considering the conditional probabilty for
\Omega_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s,a}m\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\big\}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2}{{p_{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{max,m}^{1/2}+t\quad\text{with probability larger than}

\displaystyle 1-\exp(\frac{1}{4})\cdot\exp(\frac{-n}{128}\cdot{p_{\rm min}}^{2})-|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot 2\cdot\exp\bigg(\frac{-n\cdot{p_{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot t^{2}}{4C_{max,m}^{2}}\bigg).

Letting
t^{2}=\frac{4C_{max,m}^{2}}{n\cdot{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\log(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}})
for arbitrary
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
, given that we have sufficiently large
n
such that

\displaystyle\exp(\frac{1}{4})\cdot\exp(\frac{-n}{128}\cdot{p_{\rm min}}^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0},

(35)

we have following with probability larger than
1-\delta_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s,a}m\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\big\}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{{p_{\rm min}}}}\cdot\max\{C_{max,m},1\}\cdot O\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\cdot\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)}\bigg\}.

Note that we have following under
\Omega_{0}
that leads to
\rho(s,a)\leq 2{\hat{\rho}}(s,a)
,

\displaystyle m_{\infty}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq{\sum_{s,a}}\frac{\rho(s,a)}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot m^{2}\big\{\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(s,a),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a)\big\}=\frac{1}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\bar{m}_{\rho,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}}),

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{\rho,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}_{s,a\sim\rho}\big\{m^{2}\big(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(s,a),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a)\big)\big\}\leq 2\cdot{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}}_{s,a,\sim{\hat{\rho}}}\big\{m^{2}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t}}(s,a),\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a))\big\}

\displaystyle=2\cdot\bar{m}_{n,1}^{2}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq 8\cdot\sup_{s,a}m^{2}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s.a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\big\}\quad\text{by \eqref{ineq:sample_inaccuracybyBR},}

\displaystyle\therefore\ m_{\infty}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq\sqrt{\frac{8}{{p_{\rm min}}}}\cdot\sup_{s,a}m\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{*,t}}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}(s,a)\big\}.

Then, we have following with probability larger than
1-\delta_{0}
,

\displaystyle m_{\infty}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq\frac{1}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\max\{C_{max,m},1\}\cdot O\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\cdot\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)}\bigg\}.

Under Assumption
3.2
, we have
\eta_{\infty}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})\leq C_{surr}\cdot m_{\infty}^{\delta}({\hat{\theta}_{n,t}},{\theta_{*,t}})+\epsilon_{0}
, which allows us to bound
\eta_{\infty}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{t}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}})
for all
t\in[T]
. Now letting
n=\frac{N}{T}
, we have following with probability larger than
1-T\cdot\delta_{0}
,
\eta_{\infty}(\Upsilon_{\pi},\Upsilon_{\pi})
can be bounded by following based on (
6
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\zeta}\cdot C_{surr}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\zeta}\cdot C_{surr}\cdot\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{1}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\max\{C_{max,m},1\}\bigg)^{\delta}\cdot O\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{N/T}\cdot\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)\bigg)^{\delta/2}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-\zeta}\cdot\epsilon_{0}+\zeta^{T}\cdot\eta_{\infty}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\qquad\text{with}\quad\zeta=\gamma^{c}\quad\text{by \eqref{def:supextension}.}

under the premise that (
35
) holds with
n=N/T
.

Now we choose
T=\lfloor\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}(\frac{N}{\log(4|\mathcal{S\times A}|/\delta_{0})})\rfloor
, which also leads to
\zeta^{T}\approx(\frac{1}{N}\cdot\log(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}))^{\delta/2}
. For sufficiently large
N
, (
35
) holds with
n=N/T
and
\delta_{0}
replaced with
\delta_{0}/T
for sufficiently large
N
. Note that we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N/T}\cdot\log\bigg(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}/T}\bigg)\approx\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}\bigg(\frac{N}{\log(4|\mathcal{S\times A}|/\delta_{0})}\bigg)\cdot\bigg\{\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)+\log T\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2N}\cdot\frac{1}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\cdot\bigg\{\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)+\log\big(\frac{\delta}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\big)\bigg\}.

Thus, with probability larger than
1-\delta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{\infty}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{c}}\cdot C_{surr}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\max\{C_{max,m},1\}\bigg)^{\delta}\times

(36)

\displaystyle O\bigg(\bigg[\frac{\delta}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\cdot\bigg\{\log\big(\frac{4|\mathcal{S\times A}|}{\delta_{0}}\big)+\log\big(\frac{\delta}{c}\cdot\log_{1/\gamma}N\big)\bigg\}\bigg]^{\delta/2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{1-\gamma^{c}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}.

C.16
Parameters of Examples in Table
1

For all other examples except for the first two examples of Table
1
, the values of
C_{surr}
and
C_{max,m}
are the same with what we have derived in the proofs of corollaries of Appendix
B
. For their values, refer to the proof of each corollary of its subsections (which are shown in Appendices
C.8
–
C.14
). Here, we will only present the first two examples of Table
1
, whose values of
C_{surr}
and
C_{max,m}
are different from those of Appendix
B
.

For
m=l_{2}
, we need to assume that return distributions are 1-dimension and bounded, i.e.,
Z(s,a;\theta)\in[a,b]
for all
s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A}
and
\theta\in\Theta
. By Lemma
C.7
and
\mathcal{E}=2l_{2}^{2}
, we have
C_{surr}=(b-a)^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}
and
\delta=1/p
,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(b-a)^{2p-1}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{p}^{2p}(P,Q)\leq 2\cdot l_{2}^{2}(P,Q),\quad\therefore\ \bigg(\frac{1}{b-a}\bigg)^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{p}(P,Q)\leq l_{2}^{1/p}(P,Q).

We also have
C_{max,m}=C\cdot(b-a)^{1/2}
.

For
m={\rm MMD}_{\beta}
, we need to assume
D<\infty
(where
D>0
is the diameter of support). Since the corresponding kernel can be considered as
k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=-\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|^{\beta}
, we have
C_{max,m}=C\cdot D^{\beta/2}
. We have
C_{surr}=1
and
\delta=1
since
\eta=m
.

C.17
Comparison and examples on Cramer FDE

C.17.1
Comparison between Cramer FDE and FLE

Although FLE
[
61
]
did not suggest a probability bound for
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}
-inaccuracy, we can utilize their key results (e.g., their Lemma 4.9) to build such bound. With probability larger than
1-\delta
, we have the following with in a single iteration based on
\mathcal{D}_{t}
with
|\mathcal{D}_{t}|=n
:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{t}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{{\hat{\theta}_{n,t-1}}})\leq C_{cover}^{1/2p}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{1-\gamma}\cdot\bigg[\frac{10}{n}\cdot\log\bigg\{\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|,(n|\mathcal{Y}|)^{-1}\big)\bigg/\delta\bigg\}\bigg]^{1/2p}.

Here,
\|\cdot\|
can be any norm that can be defined on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}
. For fair comparison, let us use

\displaystyle\|f(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{L_{1},\infty}:=\sup_{s,a}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|f(z|s,a)|\mathrm{d}z,

which is what we already defined in (NS4) (or Appendix
C.8
). Skipping algebraic details, this leads to following with appropriate choice of
T
with respect to the size of whole data
N=|\mathcal{D}|
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\lesssim\tilde{O}_{P}\big(N^{\frac{-1}{2p}(1-\alpha)}\big)\quad\text{under}\quad\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},\epsilon)\lesssim\epsilon^{-\alpha}.

Assuming the same condition, we can also bound
\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},\epsilon)
, where
\mathcal{F}_{\Theta}
represents the conditional cdf family, as we have mentioned in Appendix
C.8
. Letting
M=\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},\epsilon)
, there exists pairs of functions
\{[l_{i}(\cdot|\cdots),u_{i}(\cdot|\cdots)]\}_{i=1}^{M}
that serve as
\epsilon
-bracketing of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}
. Then, let us define

\displaystyle L_{i}(y|s,a):=\int_{a}^{y}l_{i}(z|s,a)\mathrm{d}z\quad\&\quad U_{i}(y|s,a):=\int_{a}^{y}u_{i}(z|s,a)\mathrm{d}z.

Now arbitrarily pick
F_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)\in\mathcal{F}_{\Theta}
, and let its corresponding conditional pdf as
f_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)
. There exists a bracketing pair
[l_{k}(\cdot|\cdots),u_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)]
such that
l_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)\leq f_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)\leq u_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)
and
\|l_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)-u_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{L_{1},\infty}\leq\epsilon
. Then, not only do we have
L_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)\leq F_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots)\leq U_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)
, but also

\displaystyle\|L_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)-U_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{L_{1},\infty}=\sup_{s,a}\int_{a}^{b}\bigg|L_{k}(y|s,a)-U_{k}(y|s,a)\bigg|\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{a}^{y}\bigg|l_{k}(z|s,a)-u_{k}(z|s,a)\bigg|\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}y\leq(b-a)\cdot\|l_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)-u_{k}(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{L_{1},\infty}\leq(b-a)\cdot\epsilon.

This further implies
\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},(b-a)\cdot\epsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},\epsilon)\lesssim\epsilon^{-\alpha}
, thereby satisfying the condition of (NS4). This allows us to apply Corollary
B.1
, yielding
\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{p,d_{\pi},p}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{T}},\Upsilon_{\pi})=\tilde{O}_{P}(N^{-\frac{1}{p(2+\alpha)}})
.

C.17.2
Example 1: Linear MDP

Let us assume the following model with
\Theta=(\mathcal{F})^{K}
, where
\mathcal{F}
is a collection of cdf’s of random variables bounded in
[a,b]
. Here, we can see
\theta
as an element
\mathbf{H}(\cdot)=(H_{1}(\cdot),\cdots,H_{K}(\cdot))^{\intercal}
with each
H_{k}\in\mathcal{F}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}:=\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}\in(\mathcal{P}[a,b])^{\mathcal{S\times A}}\text{ with }\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\text{ has cdf }F_{\theta}(z|s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\mathbf{H}(z)\rangle\ \bigg|\ \mathbf{H}\in(\mathcal{F})^{K}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\mathbf{1}_{K}\rangle=1\quad\&\quad\phi_{k}(s,a)\geq 0\text{ for }\forall s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\bm{\phi}}:=\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim\rho}\big\{{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),{\bm{\phi}}^{\intercal}(s,a)\big\}\ \text{ is non-singular.}

(37)

Note that tabular setting (
|\mathcal{S\times A}|<\infty
) is a special case of linear MDP model. This is the case with
\phi_{k}(s,a)=\mathbf{1}(s,a=(s,a)_{k})
with
k=1,\cdots,|\mathcal{S\times A}|
.

Assumption
3.1
is satisfied when the transition is expressed as a linear combination of features
{\tilde{p}}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\bm{\theta}(r,s^{\prime})\rangle
with
\bm{\theta}(r,s^{\prime})=(\theta_{1}(r,s^{\prime}),\cdots,\theta_{K}(r,s^{\prime}))^{\intercal}
being valid densities over
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}
. Also assume Assumption
C.3
.

For (NS4), we can use Theorem 2.7.5 of
[
53
]
to bound
\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,L_{1}},\epsilon)
. Letting
Q={\rm Unif}[a,b]
, we have the following,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1}},\epsilon)=\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1}(Q)},\frac{\epsilon}{b-a})\leq C(b-a)\cdot\epsilon^{-1}.

where we have used
\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L_{1}}=(b-a)\cdot\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L_{1}(Q)}
in the first equality.

Arbitrarily choose
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{H}_{1}},\Upsilon_{\mathbf{H}_{2}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}
and their corresponding cdf’s
F_{\mathbf{H}_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\mathbf{H}_{1}(\cdot)\rangle
and
F_{\mathbf{H}_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\mathbf{H}_{2}(\cdot)\rangle
.
Then, we can derive the following with
\mathcal{F}[a,b]
being the cdf’s of bounded random variables in
[a,b]
,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{L_{1},\infty},\epsilon)\leq K\cdot\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}[a,b],\|\cdot\|_{L_{1}},\epsilon)\leq CK(b-a)\cdot\epsilon^{-1}.

The first inequality can be verified as follows. Let the bracketing pairs of
\mathcal{F}
be
[l_{1}(\cdot),u_{1}(\cdot)],\cdots[l_{M}(\cdot),u_{M}(\cdot)]
where
M=\log_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{1},\epsilon)
. Make
K
copies for each pair, so that we have
[l_{1}^{(k)}(\cdot),u_{1}^{(k)}(\cdot)],\cdots[l_{M}^{(k)}(\cdot),u_{M}^{(k)}(\cdot)]
for each
k\in[K]
. This means that we can construct
M^{K}
tuples of

\displaystyle\bigg([l_{j_{1}}^{(1)},u_{j_{1}}^{(1)}],\cdots,[l_{j_{K}}^{(K)},u_{j_{1}}^{(K)}]\bigg)\quad\text{where }j_{1},\cdots,j_{K}\in[M].

Pick an arbitrary
F(\cdot|\cdots)\in\mathcal{F}_{\Theta}
, and it has corresponding
\mathbf{H}=(H_{1},\cdots,H_{K})
such that
F(\cdot|s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),\mathbf{H}(\cdot)\rangle
. Since
